# PHASE 2 — PER-FORMULA LEDGER

**Status** FIRST PASS. **8.7 % of the required determinations are made; 91.3 % are UNVERIFIED.**
Nothing in this file marks a Phase-2 requirement COMPLETE.

P02-107 requires a verdict per formula and P02-108-111 fix the vocabulary to
**PASS / FAIL / WARNING / UNVERIFIED**. P02-112 forbids *"probably correct"*. This ledger
therefore assigns a verdict ONLY where evidence already held determines one, and UNVERIFIED
with a stated reason everywhere else. **UNVERIFIED here means not yet examined, not benign.**

**Scope** the **1,288 retained engineering relations** (register class C1) x the **20 questions**
of P02-087-106 = **25,760 determinations** required.

**Tooling** `scratchpad/reg_parse.mjs` + `build_p2_ledger.mjs` + `emit_ledger.mjs`. Neither runs
the engine nor inspects any formula's numerical output, so no part of this is self-certifying.
The register is parsed by taking the columns before `equation as coded` from the left and those
after it from the right, because equations legitimately contain `||` and a naive split is corrupt
(N-21). The parse is validated against the controlled vocabularies of the trailing columns:
`confidence` resolves to exactly 3 values, `impl` to 1, `unitVerif` to 5, and the `unitVerif`
split reproduces the register's independently recorded 165/198/135/6/784 exactly.

## 2. What the twenty answered questions rest on

- **Q3 units (P02-089)** — PASS where the register resolved the units and dimensional
  propagation found them consistent (DECLARED or DERIVED). **FAIL on the 6 rows carrying a
  recorded dimensional CONFLICT.** UNVERIFIED for AMBIGUOUS and UNRESOLVED, which is the
  **28.2 % units-resolution cap**; its only lever is source-side naming, a production change
  that is not authorised (N-18).
- **Q9 duplication (P02-095)** — fully determined, the only question that is. Decided by exact
  equation-shape match inside the retained set. Note this asks only about duplication WITHIN
  the retained relations; the register's 422 C7 sites are duplicate implementations that were
  excluded from C1 by the classification rule, and are therefore not re-counted here.
- **Q10 coefficient sourcing (P02-096)** — PASS on DIRECTLY STATED and MEASURED FROM SOURCE.
  **WARNING on the 25 rows declared an ENGINEERING ASSUMPTION**: declared is not sourced.
  UNVERIFIED on the **944 rows (73.3 %) whose source is UNVERIFIED** — this figure was carried
  forward unverified in N-21 and is **now re-derived and confirmed** at 944/1288.
- **Q20 zero/negative/NaN (P02-106)** — **ASYMMETRIC BY DESIGN, and it yields no PASS.** The
  absence of any guard near a site is evidence the case is unhandled, so it is recorded as a
  **WARNING (253 relations)**. The presence of a guard does NOT show that it covers this
  relation, so it yields UNVERIFIED rather than PASS. A mechanical scan cannot earn a PASS here.
  **Three of the 253 are hand-verified rather than scanned** (`gearlayout.js` l.173, 176, 181):
  the zero-denominator fallback there does catch 0, but NaN is falsy, so a NaN *numerator* passes
  straight through to the output. Mutation returns NaN, and an undefined nose-wheel count returns
  a plausible-looking 1,137.2 kg against an 81.6 kg baseline. A guard that is present and does not
  cover the case is the WARNING condition, not the UNVERIFIED one. Latent, not live:
  `landinggear.js` always supplies the wheel counts.

- **Q1, Q4, Q5 and Q11 — hand-audited, not scanned, and only where the evidence is in hand.** The
  first determinations on these four questions come from the `src/engine/gearlayout.js` audit:
  **19 relations, 48 determinations**, each traceable to a stated basis in its row's notes and to a
  probe kept in the session scratchpad. Nothing here is mechanical, and nothing outside that one
  file is claimed. **Q10 was deliberately not touched**: the gate binds it to the register's
  `source` column with no escape clause, the register records those rows UNVERIFIED, and editing
  the register so that it agrees with a conclusion drawn from it is the F-42 error.
  - **Q1 mathematical expression (P02-087)** — **19 PASS**, earned three ways: exhaustive
    enumeration over a closed domain (the tyre table, 11 of 11 designations parse to the correct
    section width), algebra (the moment balance `x_main = (x_cg - f x_nose) / (1 - f)` inverted
    correctly), and an exact identity (the mass partition reproduces `landingGear().mass` to
    1.9e-16 on all six configurations).
  - **Q4 sign convention (P02-090)** — **17 PASS**: x measured aft from the nose and z positive up,
    with `x_nose < x_cg < x_main` and static clearance > 0 on all six configurations. The two
    tyre-width relations are **left UNVERIFIED on purpose** — a section width is a positive
    magnitude carrying no sign convention, and a vacuous PASS is still an unearned determination.
  - **Q5 reference conditions (P02-091)** — **1 WARNING**. The vertical criterion requires static
    clearance to cover the fitted strut stroke, but `landinggear.js`'s energy balance treats tyre
    deflection as a **separate co-absorber**, so at full compression the aircraft descends by the
    stroke *plus* the tyre deflection. WARNING and not FAIL, because that term is never the binding
    one: the strut-length term binds on all six configurations with 0.18-0.23 m of margin, and no
    configuration strikes the ground. **An incomplete criterion is not a failure.**
  - **Q11 hidden double-counting (P02-097)** — **8 PASS**, on the mass relations only, where the
    partition identity is the evidence. The geometric relations are left UNVERIFIED because the
    question does not bite on them and nothing measured here would settle it.

## 3. The two register columns that already state Phase 2 is undone

Independent of anything measured here, the register itself records for **all 1,288** relations:

- `validation` = **"NOT VALIDATED — Phase 2"**
- `indep verif` = **"NO — no independent calculation"**

So Phase 2 has no independently recomputed relation anywhere in the engine. That is consistent
with 0 of 112 requirements COMPLETE and with the no-self-certification rule: an independent
calculation is exactly what has not been done.

On a tapered swept wing the half-chord sweep is smaller - 21.89 against 25.03 deg on the default transport, 13.00 against 16 on the business jet. A declared substitution for a defined symbol is not that symbol; 7: WARNING - the Prandtl-Glauert factor is floored at beta^2 >= 1e-6 and the Mach fed to it is clipped at 0.95, so the relation returns a finite slope in a transonic region where the correction has no validity. The module's units match all six; 4: dn is positive for positive inputs and enters the envelope as 1 + dn on the positive branch and 1 - dn on the negative, which is the symmetric pair 25.341(a)(5)(i) requires for 'positive and negative gusts'; 7: WARNING - the closed form is applied where the regulation requires something else. 25.341(a)(1) requires that loads 'must be determined by dynamic analysis' taking account of unsteady aerodynamics and all significant structural degrees of freedom, and 25.341(a)(3) requires that 'a sufficient number of gust gradient distances in the range 30 feet to 350 feet must be investigated'. This evaluates one algebraic form at one gradient distance. | FORMULA-STRUCT-023 | `src/classes/aircraft/flight-envelope.js:201` | P | W | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | W | P | U | U | 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: the seed and floor of the V_C fixed point. The 1.5 is UNSOURCED and can govern V_C at high wing loading; see F-57; 2: WARNING - the symbol is named a FALLBACK for V_C and the comment at l.193 calls it 'the REGULATORY MINIMUM, not a guess at the real V_C', but the expression is a maximum of that minimum AND an unsourced 1.5 V_S1 floor. The name and the comment describe only one of the two. See F-57; 17: WARNING - genuinely circular, BY REGULATION, and resolved rather than hidden. 25.335(d)(1) makes V_B a function of V_C, and 25.335(a)(2) makes V_C a function of V_B. The module solves the pair with an explicit fixed point at l.202-209 and says so in its comment. The circularity is real and is a property of the rule, not of the implementation; that it is resolved correctly is recorded under question 18; 18: PROVEN STABLE, not assumed. The V_C fixed point at l.202-209 was re-implemented with instrumentation and measured: the iteration map is a CONTRACTION, with abs(f'(V_C*)) = 0.1935 transport, 0.2438 bizjet, 0.2404 turboprop, all well inside the < 1 a contraction requires. Seeded from 0.01 V_S1 to 1000 V_S1 - seven seeds spanning five orders of magnitude - every run converges to the same V_C within 5.7e-7 kt, so the answer does not depend on the initial guess. It takes 12 to 15 iterations against the cap of 40, i.e. 2.7 times headroom, and a hostile case at 5 lb/ft^2 with a 60 ft chord seeded at 1e6 kt still converged in 12. The one gap is reporting, not stability: the loop carries no convergence flag, so exhausting the cap would be indistinguishable from converging. See F-58 |

The resultant matches the applied shear in magnitude AND direction at every boom count tested, 3 to 65; 5: the reference datum for theta is declared at line 95 as the starboard horizontal and the implementation is consistent with it - the flow is largest at theta = 0 on the neutral axis and zero at +/- pi/2 on the load axis. Note that the research note FUSELAGE-STRUCTURE.md section 2.1 states the same falsifiable check as (S_y/pi R) sin(theta): that is the identical function measured from the vertical, and the note's own build record states the cos form the module implements, so the datum is documented rather than in conflict; 7: WARNING - two validity conditions, one met and one not. Every arrow runs from inputs to outputs and no relation appears in its own dependency cone; 19: exactly homogeneous of degree 1, as a perimeter must be -- scaling both semi-axes by k scales the result by k, measured 2.000000000000 at k=2 and 10.000000000000 at k=10; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified rather than scanned. three unguarded holes, all in the exported function and none reachable from the module's own call site. ellipsePerimeter(0, 0) returns NaN because h is 0/0. ellipsePerimeter(-1, -1) returns -6.283185, a NEGATIVE perimeter, silently. Any inverted axis with b/a < 0 large enough to put h above 4/3 makes sqrt(4 - 3h) imaginary, so ellipsePerimeter(1, -3) with h = 4 and ellipsePerimeter(1, -0.5) with h = 9 both return NaN. (24), the report's single continuous force form, as 2.4 times the bracket x - Cz(1 - exp(-0.6x/Cz)) with x the deflection ratio delta/w. Reproduced against an independently written transcription at abs difference 0.0e+0 over 4,000 values of x in (0, 4], and the large-x asymptote 2.4(x - Cz) is exact at x = 5. Strictly increasing and positive throughout, measured; 2: x is TN 4110's deflection ratio delta/w, deflection over SECTION WIDTH, and Cz is the report's type constant. The report's own non-dimensionalisation of the left side, F_z over (p + 0.08 p_r) w sqrt(w d), is legible in the vendored text and is deliberately absent from the module, because the efficiency is a ratio in which it cancels; 4: the force is positive and strictly increasing on (0, 4], measured at 4,000 points, which is what a tyre carrying load must do; 7: applied well inside its range. The bracket is smooth and monotone for every positive x, and the deflection ratios the shipped tyre table can produce span 0.018750 to 0.378378 across all 294 rows, measured; 8: WARNING -- an empirical constant is carried one tyre type beyond the data it was measured on, declared but not avoided. Cz is empirical: the vendored TN 4110 text states twice that Cz = 0.02 for type I tires and 0.03 for types III and VII. The module applies the type III and VII value to EVERY row, including the 27 radial tyres in the table, and says so in a radialNote recording that radials post-date the 1957 report and that it offers no radial figure. That is honest about the extrapolation and better than inventing a radial constant, but it is still an empirical constant applied outside its measured population, and nothing bounds the error it introduces; 17: not circular. The module is a single forward pass with no fixed point: the force function depends only on its two arguments, the efficiency on the deflection ratio and the type constant, the deflection ratio on three tabulated lengths, and the stroke on inputs all fixed before it is called. No relation appears in its own dependency cone, and the caller supplies gravity rather than the module reaching back for it; 19: dimensionless in a dimensionless argument, so scale-free by construction: scaling a tyre's deflection and section width together leaves x unchanged and therefore the force function unchanged; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified, and the previous note on this row was the analyser rather than the code; see F-65. The enclosing function DOES guard, at l.92, and the scan could not see it. Three holes remain, hand-verified: the guard returns 0 rather than refusing, so a NaN deflection yields a FORCE OF ZERO, which is a plausible number a consumer will use; the Cz argument is unguarded, so Cz = 0 silently returns the un-offset line and a negative Cz returns a negative force, measured -10.0137 at x = 0.25; and x = Infinity returns Infinity. Latent: the only in-module call passes a positive ratio and the frozen constant |

MIL-L-8552C section 3.5.2 obtains its absorbed area A *from the data obtained during a drop test*, which is a DYNAMIC, hysteretic loop; this integral is of TN 4110's STATIC force-deflection curve. TN 4110 carries both, listing a static and a dynamic force-deflection variation and a whole section of hysteresis properties, and LANDING-GEAR.md records its vertical hysteresis parameter at about 0.1 while saying it is *not the ratio of absorbed energy to L_max delta*. The static route is not an invention: it is exactly option (b) of that note's own gap 1, *integrate the TN 4110 static force-deflection curve to derive eta_t honestly*. So the choice is sourced and declared as applying a strut definition to a tyre, but the static-against-dynamic difference is not stated anywhere in the module; 7: the resulting efficiency is confined to about [0.445, 0.5] for EVERY positive deflection ratio, because both limits are one half and the interior minimum is 0.445071 at X = 0.091769, located by golden section. Over the whole 294-row table the efficiency spans 0.448371 to 0.468781, so every tabulated tyre lands inside that analytic band and none approaches its edge; 8: WARNING -- as 179; Cz enters this term directly and the 27 radial rows integrate a type VII constant; 11: the dimensionless factor 2.4 appears in both the absorbed area and the rectangle and cancels exactly, verified by computing the efficiency from the full 2.4-bearing curve and from the module and finding them equal to 5.6e-16 or better. So the scaling is applied once and then divided out, not applied twice; 17: not circular. The module is a single forward pass with no fixed point: the force function depends only on its two arguments, the efficiency on the deflection ratio and the type constant, the deflection ratio on three tabulated lengths, and the stroke on inputs all fixed before it is called. No relation appears in its own dependency cone, and the caller supplies gravity rather than the module reaching back for it; 19: as 179. This is the property that makes the efficiency a curve in one variable rather than a surface over tyre size, which is the whole reason the derivation closes; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified, and the previous note on this row was the analyser rather than the code; see F-65. A guard is present at l.102 and the quotient is additionally protected at l.106, and the scan saw neither. The hand-verified hole is conditioning rather than a missing test: the three terms of this numerator cancel to leading order as X falls, so the coded form returns 0.5008 at X = 1e-8, 21.67 at 1e-10, 6206.78 at 1e-12 and NEGATIVE values below 1e-13, against an analytic bound of (0, 0.5). Independently, at L = 1 it agrees with the collapsed form V^2/(2 g eta_s N) - (eta_t/eta_s) d_t to 6.2e-16, and with d_t = 0 it returns V^2/(2 g eta_s N) exactly; 2: V the design sink speed, g gravity, L the lift-to-weight ratio, d_t the tyre deflection at the design load, eta_t the tyre efficiency, N the reaction factor, each matching the definitions under eq. (23). g is injected by the caller, so this module holds no constant of its own and cannot drift from the engine's gravity; 4: the stroke came out positive on all 3,540 resolved combinations with physical inputs, and the sign of each term is the one eq. (22) assigns it; 5: the reference condition is the design landing case and it is correctly carried: V comes from the sink-speed module citing 14 CFR 25.473(a)(2), and L is the lift-to-weight ratio at impact, at most 1.0 for Part 25. Measured, all three Part 25 classes arrive at L = 1.0 exactly, giving a denominator of 1.5 against eta_s N of 1.5, and the trainer never reaches this relation at all, refusing earlier for want of a wheelbase. NOTE ON THE CITATION the module carries: its src string points at LANDING-GEAR.md *section 1.4b eq. (23)*, but 1.4b is the section quoting FAA AC 25.723-1's effective-weight equation, and (22) and (23) are in section 1.4c, which labels them *algebra on primary results, not a quoted equation*. The equation and its derived status are identified correctly; only the subsection letter is wrong, and it points at the section that quotes a primary rather than the one that derives. Raised as F-66; 7: inherits 186's validity condition. Within it the solution is exact, which the residual against (22) establishes rather than assumes; 11: the tyre deflection appears TWICE in eq. (23), as (1 - L) d_t and as -eta_t N d_t, and that is not a double count: the first is the work the unbalanced weight does while the aircraft settles through the tyre's travel, the second is the energy the tyre itself absorbs, and they are opposite sides of the balance. The residual test against (22) is what establishes this, because a double count would leave a residual proportional to d_t and the measured residual is 4.4e-15 of the kinetic term across 3,540 cases. At L = 1 the first term vanishes identically, which the collapsed form shows; 17: not circular. The module is a single forward pass with no fixed point: the force function depends only on its two arguments, the efficiency on the deflection ratio and the type constant, the deflection ratio on three tabulated lengths, and the stroke on inputs all fixed before it is called. No relation appears in its own dependency cone, and the caller supplies gravity rather than the module reaching back for it; 19: scales as the physics requires and was measured doing so. The stroke varies exactly as V squared, confirmed at twice and three times the design speed against the closed form at relative 0.0e+0 to 2.2e-16. It is deliberately NOT homogeneous in g, because only the kinetic term carries gravity while the two tyre terms do not, and with d_t set to zero it collapses to V^2/(2 g eta_s N) exactly; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified, and the previous note on this row was the analyser rather than the code; see F-65. As 186, and this is where the unvalidated inputs land: eta_t = NaN and d_t = NaN each return a NaN stroke with resolved true, eta_t = 5 returns a stroke of -0.344884 m and L = 5 one of -0.002426 m, both reported resolved, and d_t = -0.1 m returns a positive stroke from a negative deflection. A negative stroke is worse than a NaN because it is a number a consumer will format. Latent: gear.js supplies eta_t bounded in (0, 0.5), eta_s frozen at 0.75 and d_t from the tyre table, and the three Part 25 classes measure 255 to 283 mm, all positive. See F-63 |

The comment calls this ArduPilot's cascade, limited by the published acceleration maxima. ArduPilot's sqrt_controller (control.cpp) is linear up to a/P^2 and then sqrt(2 a (abs(e) - a/(2 P^2))); the module takes min(P e, sqrt(2 a abs(e))), linear up to 2 a/P^2. This output is used as a MOMENT in N m (it is the moment part of the wrench the allocator turns into rotor thrusts). The gains are ArduPilot's EDU450 rate gains, whose units are s/rad and whose output is a NORMALISED mixer command: AC_AttitudeControl_Multi passes update_all(...) to _motors.set_roll(), and AP_MotorsMatrix scales roll factors to +-0.5. One unit of command is therefore about 0.5 fMax sum abs(y_i) N m - about 2.55 N m on a 450-class quad (0.225 m arm, 8 N motors), 21.2 N m at 1 m with 30 N motors, 127 N m for a 1.5 m heavy-lift with 120 N motors - and the module's rate loop is that many times softer than ArduPilot's. The verdict that reads the ratio also misnames it: for a cruise speed from 211.54 to 274.99 kt, below the 275 kt boundary, it prints 'cruise is AT OR ABOVE the reference boundary', measured; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 4: a ratio of two positive speeds. A zero or negative cruise speed is refused at the site (vKt > 0) and returns no ratio, measured at 0 and -10 m/s; 5: WARNING, from the primary. Acree's 335 and 275 kt are true airspeeds at sea-level standard conditions, rotor at 457 rpm (76 % of hover design rpm), limited-power trim (Acree 1999, p.2 and Appendix). None of these conditions is carried into the result, and the caller's cruise speed is compared with the sea-level boundary whatever the cruise altitude or rotor speed; 7: WARNING, measured. The reference is one airframe, the XV-15, applied to every tiltrotor, hybrid and side-by-side layout, which the module states. Within the published pair the choice is conservative: a wing of t/c 0.20 to 0.229, nearer the thick wing, receives the thin wing's 275 kt. Below the thin wing it is not: thinnerThanReference is set but the verdict never reads it, so t/c 0.08 at 60 m/s reads 'cruise sits far below the reference boundary' at a ratio of 2.36 against a boundary the module itself calls 'at least this bad'. The site's own test refuses a zero, negative, NaN, infinite, null, empty or absent speed and reports 'cruise speed unknown', which conflates an invalid speed with an unknown one, and a negative speed still prints vCruiseKt -19.4. | FORMULA-REG-001 | `src/classes/aircraft/compliance.js:118` | P | P | U | P | F | U | W | U | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: the inversion of Scholz (5.14), T/W = nE/(nE-1) (1/E + gamma), for gamma at the design T/W, recomputed from the matching chart's OWN definition of each line (transport/size.js:200,203) rather than from this shortcut. The second segment reproduces at relative 2.2e-16 or better on nine configurations - three engine counts by three fuel methods - and on the bizjet, and back-solving the gradient at the chart's own twReq returns the regulation's 0.024 to 6.9e-18. The expression is right; where it is applied is not - see 5; 2: req is the 25.121 minimum, read verbatim from the vendored 14 CFR 25.121 text - 2.4/2.7/3.0 % at (b)(1) and 2.1/2.4/2.7 % at (d)(1), the same tables as transport/size.js:51-52; twDesign is all-engine sea-level static thrust over MTOW weight, which is Scholz's T_TO/(m_MTO g); twReq is the chart's line; nE is clamped to 2-4, the only engine counts 25.121 tabulates, and agrees with the chart's unclamped nE on every live configuration; 4: positive margin means compliant, uniformly, and achieved - required equals the margin exactly; 5: FAIL, hand-verified against the primary. Scholz (5.22)-(5.24), vendored, states the missed-approach line at the maximum LANDING mass and converts it to take-off mass by m_ML/m_MTO, which transport/size.js:203 does. Inverting that line for gamma therefore needs the T/W divided by the same ratio, and this relation omits it: the B-17 margin it reports is lambda times the true one, exactly. Measured 16.00 % low on the transport (lambda 0.84) at every engine count and fuel method, 10.50 % low on the bizjet (0.895); achieved 7.79 % against a true 8.87 %, and 16.66 % against 18.36 %. The verdict cannot flip, because lambda > 0 preserves the sign of the surplus, so it is conservative and LOW. The module separately DECLARES its static-thrust basis as optimistic; that is not this defect. F-84; 7: WARNING, measured. Scholz (5.17) substitutes the climb gradient for sin(gamma) because 'the angle is small'; true at the requirement (0.03 % at 2.4 %) and no longer at the achieved values this row reports - the bizjet's 17.33 % second-segment figure is sin(gamma), whose tangent, the regulation's gradient, is 17.60 %, 1.54 % apart; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 11: the one-engine-inoperative factor is applied exactly ONCE. twReq already carries nE/(nE-1), the inversion applies (nE-1)/nE once to the surplus, and the back-solve at twReq lands on the requirement to 7e-18; 17: not circular. The design T/W is itself the maximum over the chart's lines (transport/size.js:424), but this relation reads the converged result after sizing and feeds nothing back; 19: affine in the design T/W with slope exactly (nE-1)/nE, measured at relative 0.0e+0 for two, three and four engines on two successive steps; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. l.114 refuses a non-finite T/W on either side and the row becomes NOT EVALUATED, measured for NaN and Infinity. Not covered: a non-integer or NaN engine count reaches GRAD[nE] as undefined and the row reports FAIL with a NaN achieved value and NaN margin - measured on a synthetic result, and unreachable from the engine, measured: sizing the transport at 2.5 engines gives a NaN twSecond from the chart, which l.114 refuses first. Zero and negative T/W give a FAIL, which is the correct answer. The turboprop never reaches l.118 at all: its chart reports the same two lines as power-to-mass (pmSecond, pmMissed) and the guard reads only twSecond, so both rows are NOT EVALUATED under the false reason that the chart does not report them. F-84 |
| FORMULA-REG-002 | `src/classes/aircraft/compliance.js:144` | P | P | P | P | P | U | U | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: newtons to kilograms by standard gravity, reproduced against the mission's own pound figure - trip/9.80665 = 15,788.2234 kg on the transport and tripLb x 0.45359237 = 15,788.2234 kg, relative 2.2e-16; the bizjet 2,981.550 kg at 0.0e+0; 2: trip is flyMission's TRIP fuel weight (transport/mission.js:242), taxi + take-off + climb + cruise + descent and NO reserve, carried in newtons because the mission is flown in newtons from a ramp weight in newtons (transport/size.js:313); the row's note says reserves are still aboard at landing, which is what 25.25(a) and this definition both mean; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 4: the trip fuel is positive and is subtracted from the take-off mass; 5: the reference conditions are the mission's own. The G divided here, 9.80665, is the G the mission multiplied by (transport/size.js:50, transport/mission.js:48), and the take-off mass is the ramp weight the mission was flown from. The resulting landing mass matches the mission's own landingWeightN/G to 0.196 kg in 63,012, 3.1e-6; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a conversion of a converged output, not circular; 19: exactly linear, doubling the trip doubles the mass; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. Number.isFinite refuses NaN and Infinity - but the refusal is a null that makes the W-1c row VANISH rather than read NOT EVALUATED, against the module's own header. That defect is already F-R21 for the turboprop; measured here it is wider: the transport loses the row under two of its three fuel methods, regulatory and loftin, because only the segment mission returns a trip in newtons. A zero or negative trip is not guarded and reports a landing mass at or above MTOW and a FAIL, measured on a synthetic result and unreachable from the mission |
| FORMULA-REG-009 | `src/engine/certification.js:145` | P | W | P | U | U | U | W | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | P | P | W | 1: the upper end of the payload bisection bracket, hi = max(1, maxPayloadKg if given, else twice the design payload, else twice 455 kg), recomputed longhand for ten inputs and reproduced on every one - 455 and an absent or null payload give 910, a 300 kg payload gives 600, zero or negative payloads and bounds floor at 1; 2: WARNING. hi, lo and best are what they claim to be, but n is not: the returned note prints n as the number of sizing evaluations while n counts only the bisection steps and omits the bound probe at hi, measured with a counting wrapper as 10 reported against 11 made at the 3,175 kg limit. The sibling maxRangeAtMTOM starts its count at 2 for exactly that reason. The lower bracket lo = 0 is assumed feasible and is never evaluated - see 7 and F-83; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 7: WARNING, measured. The bracket's lower end is OUTSIDE the sizing loop's own input domain - a zero payload throws inside a cabin display string (already F-R16), which feasible() catches as infeasible - and it is never tested. When nothing in the bracket closes the function returns that untested end as its answer: at a 500 kg limit, where even a 1 kg payload sizes to an MTOW of 771.2 kg, it reports payloadKg 0 under the note 'bisection on payload, 10 sizing evaluations', while maxRangeAtMTOM asked the same question reports 'no mission closes inside the limit - the airframe alone exceeds it'. At a range of 400 km it does the same at the default limit. F-83; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: not circular. The sizing function is injected rather than imported precisely so this module has no dependency on engine.js, and the bracket is fixed before the first evaluation; 18: stable, measured. Bisection halves the interval every step and terminates on the 1 kg tolerance or the iteration cap max(6, 22): the 910 kg bracket closes in exactly ceil(log2 910) = 10 steps, as observed. Its premise also holds - MTOW rises monotonically in payload, 0 decreases over 91 sized points from 10 to 910 kg at a mean 4.31 kg of MTOW per kg of payload - so feasibility is a single step and the bisection brackets it; 19: linear in the design payload with a factor of exactly 2 and a floor of 1, measured on 455, 300 and the absent case; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. The l.136 finite-check on the LIMIT stands as recorded in the Q20 integrity block, and nothing guards the bracket itself: a NaN payload or a NaN maxPayloadKg makes hi NaN, because Math.max(1, NaN) is NaN, the loop test (NaN - 0) > 1 is false, and the function returns payloadKg 0 with the note 'bisection on payload, 0 sizing evaluations' after making one. A NaN limitKg fails the l.136 check and is reported as 'no limit set', which conflates an invalid limit with a deliberate absence of one. No caller in src or validation reaches either: both inverse functions are exported from engine.js and consumed nowhere |

The one consumer, risk.js:299-312, explicitly grades a few per cent as a coarse nominal rather than a charge limit, so no answer changes; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a pure function of one pack record's published fields; 19: invariant to scaling capacity and energy together; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. It does on all 786 cases, so the refusal path is always the one taken and the fallback at Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER is never needed; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a pure function of the propeller's measured table and the density; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. The l.125 positivity refusal recorded by the Q20 integrity block governs the thrust request in rotorPoint, not this relation, as recorded. This relation has no density guard: a density of 0 returns a degenerate range [0, 0], a negative density an inverted range of negative thrusts, and a NaN density a range of NaN, which passes the truthiness test at l.109 because an array is truthy. The density comes from the caller's atmosphere and is finite and positive on every shipped path |
